<p id="mwCQ">The <b id="mwCg">Jonha Falls</b> (also called <b id="mwCw">Gautamdhara Falls</b>) is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterfall" title="Waterfall" id="mwDA">waterfall</a> located in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranchi_district" title="Ranchi district" id="mwDQ">Ranchi district</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India" title="India" id="mwDg">Indian</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_and_territories_of_India" title="States and territories of India" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDw">state</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jharkhand" title="Jharkhand" id="mwEA">Jharkhand</a>.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwGw" aria-labelledby="Location"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Location">Location</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jonha_Falls&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Location"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwHA">Jonha Falls is located at <span class="geo-inline" about="#mwt10" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Coord","href":"./Template:Coord"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"23"},"2":{"wt":"20"},"3":{"wt":"30"},"4":{"wt":"N"},"5":{"wt":"85"},"6":{"wt":"36"},"7":{"wt":"30"},"8":{"wt":"E"},"9":{"wt":"display:inline"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818" about="#mwt11" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Coordinates/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a rel="mw:ExtLink" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Jonha_Falls&amp;params=23_20_30_N_85_36_30_E_display:inline" class="external text"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">23°20′30″N</span> <span class="longitude">85°36′30″E</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct"><span typeof="mw:Entity">﻿</span> / <span typeof="mw:Entity">﻿</span></span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">23.34167°N 85.60833°E</span><span style="display:none"><span typeof="mw:Entity">﻿</span> / <span class="geo">23.34167; 85.60833</span></span></span></a></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_using_gadget_WikiMiniAtlas"/></span></p>

<p id="mwHg"><small id="mwHw">Note: The map alongside presents some of the notable locations in the district. All places marked in the map are linked in the larger full screen map. Jonha Fall can be seen  without using 722 steps  of stairs via Jonha Fall Resort.</small></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwIA" aria-labelledby="The_falls"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="The_falls">The falls</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jonha_Falls&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: The falls"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwIQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwIg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg/250px-Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="525" data-file-type="bitmap" height="150" width="200" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg/500px-Jonha_Jalprapat.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwIw"/></a><figcaption id="mwJA">July 2010.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwJQ">Situated at an edge of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chota_Nagpur_Plateau#Ranchi_plateau" title="Chota Nagpur Plateau" id="mwJg">Ranchi plateau</a>, the Jonha Falls is an example of a hanging valley falls. The Gunga River hangs over its master stream, Radu River and forms the falls.<sup about="#mwt14" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwJw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwKA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKQ">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKg">]</span></span></a></sup>
One has to descend 722 steps to admire the surroundings.<sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Jonha_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Jonha"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Jonha-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Jonha-2" id="mwKw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLg">]</span></span></a></sup> Water in the falls drops from a height of  <span about="#mwt15" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwLw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"43"},"2":{"wt":"m"}},"i":0}}]}'>43 metres (141</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt15"> </span><span about="#mwt15">ft)</span>.<sup about="#mwt22" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwMA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMg">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwNA">The Jonha Falls is an example of a nick point caused by rejuvenation. Knick point, also called a nick point or simply nick, represents breaks in slopes in the longitudinal profile of a river caused by rejuvenation. The break in channel gradient allows water to fall vertically giving rise to a waterfall.<sup about="#mwt27" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNw">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwOQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jonha_Falls,_Jharkhand,_India_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwOg"><img alt="Jonha Falls" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jonha_Falls,_Jharkhand,_India_01.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Jonha_Falls%2C_Jharkhand%2C_India_01.jpg/250px-Jonha_Falls%2C_Jharkhand%2C_India_01.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" data-file-type="bitmap" height="188" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Jonha_Falls%2C_Jharkhand%2C_India_01.jpg/500px-Jonha_Falls%2C_Jharkhand%2C_India_01.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwOw"/></a><figcaption id="mwPA"><b id="mwPQ">Jonha Falls</b></figcaption></figure>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwPg" aria-labelledby="Culture"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Culture">Culture</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jonha_Falls&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Culture"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwPw">There is a tourist rest house which encloses a Buddhist shrine with a deity of Lord <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautama_Buddha" title="Gautama Buddha" class="mw-redirect" id="mwQA">Gautama Buddha</a>.<sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Jonha_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Jonha"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Jonha-2" id="mwQQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRA">]</span></span></a></sup> A temple and an ashram dedicated to Buddha was built atop Gautam Pahar by the sons of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baldeo_Das_Birla" title="Baldeo Das Birla" id="mwRQ">Baldeo Das Birla</a>.<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwRg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwRw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">]</span></span></a></sup> A fair is organized in Jonha every Tuesday and Saturday.<sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Jonha_2-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Jonha"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Jonha-2" id="mwSg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTA">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwTg">Many remains of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism" id="mwTw">Buddhism</a> are present in Jonha Falls, situated on Gautam Hill, 38<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwUA"> </span>km away from the capital <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranchi" title="Ranchi" id="mwUQ">Ranchi</a>. There is an ancient temple of Lord <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautam_Buddha" title="Gautam Buddha" class="mw-redirect" id="mwUg">Gautam Buddha</a> on the hill of Jonha Falls. This temple was built hundreds of years ago by Raja Baldev Das Birla.<sup about="#mwt35" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwUw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwVw" aria-labelledby="Transport"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Transport">Transport</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jonha_Falls&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Transport"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwWA">The Jonha Falls is <span about="#mwt36" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwWQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"40"},"2":{"wt":"km"}},"i":0}}]}'>40 kilometres (25</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt36"> </span><span about="#mwt36">mi)</span> from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranchi" title="Ranchi" id="mwWg">Ranchi</a>. It is approachable by both road and train.<sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwWw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXg">]</span></span></a></sup> Jonha Station is just 1.5<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwXw"> </span>km from the fall. For travel by road, one has to take the Ranchi-Purulia Road and after travelling for about <span about="#mwt37" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwYA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"20"},"2":{"wt":"mi"}},"i":0}}]}'>20 miles (32</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt37"> </span><span about="#mwt37">km)</span> one has to travel about <span about="#mwt38" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwYQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"3"},"2":{"wt":"mi"}},"i":0}}]}'>3 miles (4.8</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt38"> </span><span about="#mwt38">km)</span> off the main road.<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwYg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZA">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwZg" aria-labelledby="See_also"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="See_also">See also</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jonha_Falls&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: See also"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<li id="mwag"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_waterfalls_in_India" title="List of waterfalls in India" id="mwaw">List of waterfalls in India</a></li>
<li id="mwbA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_waterfalls_in_India_by_height" title="List of waterfalls in India by height" id="mwbQ">List of waterfalls in India by height</a></li></ul>
